2 COR. III. 17, 18. The Lord is That Spirit, and where the Spirit of the Lord is, there is Liberty.

But we All with open Face, beholding as in a Glass the Glory of the Lord, are changed into the same Image from Glory to Glory, even as by the Spirit of the Lord.

For the clearer Understanding the True Meaning of these Words, it will be proper to consider the whole Scope of the Apostles Discourse in this Chapter. The Holy Ghost having been poured forth upon the Apostles at Pentecost in so very singular and plentiful a manner, as that, before that time, it is said comparatively, not to have been given at all, Joh. 7, 39; hence Paul in the 8th Verse of this Chapter, elegantly stiles the Gospel, by way of Eminence, The Ministration of the Spirit. And from That Consideration, through the Whole Chapter, magnifies the Doctrine of Christ, as being more clear and plain, more powerful and efficacious, more illustrious and glorious, than the Law of Moses. Ver. 3. Te are (faith he) the Epifle of Christ, ministred by Us, written not with Ink, but with the

on 2 Cor.3;17,18.

the Spirit of the Living God; not in Tables of Stone, but in fleshy Tables of the Heart: That is; The Power and Efficacy of the Gospel, is as much greater than that of the Law, as can be expresst by comparing that which is written in a Book, with that which is imprinted inwardly in the very Heart and Soul itself. The same Argument he pursues, ver. 6, God hath made us able Ministers of the New Testament, not of the Letter, but of the Spirit; for the Letter killeth, but the Spirit giveth Life: That is; The Gospel gives us those Spiritual Precepts, whereof the Legal Ordinances were but Types and Shadows; and teaches us the way to eternal Life, whereas the Rigour of the Law could end only in Mens condemnation. And from hence he proceeds to magnify the glorious Manifestation of the Gospel, by comparing it with the Glory that shined in Moses's Countenance; which, though so bright that the Children of Israel could not stedfastly behold it, yet was but temporary and transient, and only a Type or Figure of That permanent Glory of the Gospel, which was to continue for ever: Ver. 7. If the Ministration of Death, written and engraven in Stones, was glorious, so that the Children of Israel could not stedfastly behold the Face of Moses, for the Glory of his Countenance, which Glory was to be done away; How shall not the Ministration of the

N Spirit

193

194

## A Commentary

Spirit be rather glorious For if the Ministration of Condemnation be Glory, much more doth the Ministration of Righteousness, (of Fussification, it should be rendred,) exceed in Glory:——For if That which was done away was glorious, much more That which remaineth (that which is Perpetual) is glorious. And hereupon he takes occasion elegantly to describe the Blindness of the Jews after our Saviour's time, in not seeing through the Types and Figures of the Old Testament; by comparing it to the Veil which Moses put upon his Face to conceal the Brightness of it: Ver. 14, 15, 16, Their Minds (faith he) were blinded; For until this day remaineth the same Veil untaken away, in the reading of the Old Testament; which Veil is done away in Christ: But even unto this day, when Moses is read, the Veil is upon their Heart: Nevertheless when it [when the Heart of the People of the Jews] shall turn to the Lord [shall be converted to Christ.] the Veil shall be taken away: As he had said just before, ver. 14, which Veil is done away in Christ.

And then he fums up all, and concludes his whole Discourse, in the Words of the Text now before us : Ver. 17 ;

Now the Lord is that Spirit.] The Lord; that is, Christ, in or by whom, in or by whose Gospel, (he had said, ver. 14,) the Veil is done away; and to whom, (he had said,

## on 2 Cor. 3; 17, 18.

faid, ver. 16,) the Jews should be converted : This Lord, even Christ, is That Spirit which the Apostle had been speaking of through the Whole Chapter. His Meaning therefore is : The Gospel or Doctrine of Christ, is the Spirit, the End and Design of the Law, which giveth Life, or shows Men the way to Justification ; in opposition to the dead Letter and to the Rigour of the Law, which leads only to Condemnation. 'Tis that Spirit or final Intent of the Law, which is to continue for ever ; in opposition to those mere Types and Shadows, which were soon to be done away. 'Tis that Spirit or full Meaning and Signification of the Law, which is opposed to the Veil of Ignorance and partial Understanding of it.

195

[Chrift, or the Gospel of Christ, is here said to be the Spirit; after the same manner of speaking, as he is affirmed in other places of Scripture to be The Way, The Truth, The Life, The Wisdom or Power of God, Our Righteousness, and the like.]

And where the Spirit of the Lord is there is Liberty.] That is : Where this Ministration of the Spirit, (ver. 8;) this Manifestation of the Truth, (ver. 2, of the following Chapter,) prevails ; where the Gospel is received and embraced ; There is Liberty. Liberty, from the Bondage and Yoke of Ceremonies; Liberty, from the N 2 Domi-

196

## A Commentary

Dominion and Slavery of Sin ; Liberty, from the Rigour and Terrour of the Law, from the Ministration of Condemnation, ver. 9, from the Ministration of Death, ver. 7; But most immediately, Liberty from that Veil of Ignorance and Obscurity, which remained upon the Hearts, and perplexed the Understandings of the unbelieving Jews, when the Old Testament was read to them: In opposition to which, the Apostle argues, ver. 12, that WE use great plainness of Speech, and not as Moses which put a Veil over his Face.

But We All with open Face.] This is not said in opposition to what went before, but by way of Explication of, or Inference from, the Words immediately fore-going. It should not therefore have been rendred [BUT,] but [AND.] Where the Spirit of the Lord is, where the Gospel prevails, There is Liberty; AND We all, or, And Therefore we all, all true Christians, do with open Face, not through an obscure Veil, as did the Jews, behold the Glory of the Lord.

With open Face.] That is, clearly, plainly, and distinctly: Not in Types and Shadows, not in obscure Glimpses and feint Representations, not in remote Hints and distant Prospects; but with a full and direct View, an immediate Intuition, as of the Substance and reality, of things present and actually

## on 2 Cor. 3; 17, 18.

197

actually before us. We behold the Mystery of God in Christ, not as the Children of Israel saw the Brightness of Moses's Countenance through the Veil, (which is what the Apostle here alludes to;) but with open Face, as Moses himself is described to have seen the Lord, when the Lord spake unto him Face to Face, as a Man speaketh unto his Friend, Exod. 33, 11.

Beholding the Glory of the Lord.] That is; the Clear and Glorious Manifestation of the Will of God by the Gospel. For so the Gospel is stiled, The Riches of Gods GLORY, Rom. 9, 23; the Riches of the GLORY of this Mystery, Col. 1, 27; the GLORY as of the only-begotten of the Father, full of Grace and Truth, Joh. 1, 14; the Light of the GLORIOUS Gospel of Christ, who is the Image of God, 2 Cor. 4, 4; And ver. 6, The Light of the Knowledge of the GLORY of God, shining in our Heart. The Words of that whole Verse, are very remarkable: God, who hath commanded the Light to shine out of Darkness, (that is, who manifested his Glory originally in the First Creation of Things,) hath shined in our Hearts, (that is, hath manifested his Glory the second time no less confpicuously in our Redemption,) to give the Light of the Knowledge of the GLORY of God, in the Face of Jesus Christ. And these two last Expressions; Christ's being the IMAGE of God;

198

33

## A Commentary

and the Light of the Knowledge of God's Glory shining upon us in the FACE (or in the Person) of Christ; open to us the Ground and Meaning of that Similitude, which the Apostle here interpoes;

Beholding, as in a GLASS, the Glory of the Lord.] That which he hereby intended to express, is; that in Christ, who is the Image of the Invisible God, and the Great Revealer of his Will, we clearly and plainly behold the whole Pleasure of God towards us. For the Father, no Man hath seen at any time; no Man Hath seen, nor Can see; but the only-begotten Son, which is in the bosom of the Father, He hath declared him; And hath declared him so plainly, that he who hath seen ME, faith our Saviour, has seen the FATHER, Joh. 14, 9.

There is a Phrase very like to this in the Text before us, used in a contrary Sense by the same Apostle, I Cor. 13, 12, Now we see through a GLASS darkly, but Then Face to Face. In which Passage; seeing THROUGH a GLASS, signifies seeing darkly or obscurely, in opposition to beholding plainly, Face to Face: But here, in the present Text, beholding as IN a GLASS, signifies on the contrary, seeing clearly or plainly; and is the very same as, beholding Face to Face. The Words in the Original, are in Both places more expressive, than in the Translation; and show plainly the Reason

## on 2 Cor 3; 17, 18.

of This different Signification. In One place, the Word, which we render, Glass, signifies a Perspective-glass, [βλέπομεν διεσπίλρει] which brings distant things into the Reach indeed of our Sight, but still very obscurely, imperfectly, and indistinctly; and does therefore very elegantly, and by a most proper Similitude, express That View of a Future State, which we have by Faith and not by Sight. But now in This Other place, the Word which we render, Glass, [κατοωιλειζόμενοι] signifies a Mirroir or Looking-glass, which on the contrary represents things plainly and distinctly, Face to Face; and therefore no less elegantly represents that clear Light of the Knowledge of the Glory and Will of God, which shines to us (faith the Apostle) in the FACE of Jesus Christ.

199

Are changed into the same Image.] That is: As Christ is, by Nature, the perfect Image of God; so We, by communication of Light and Knowledge from him, and by the Practise of Righteousness and true Holiness in Obedience to him and Imitation of him, are transformed into the same Image. As Christ is, by Nature, the Son of God; so We, by Adoption and by the Fruits of the Spirit dwelling in us, have This Love bestowed upon us, that We also should be called The Sons of God: For of his Fulness have we all received, and Grace for Grace, N 4 Joh.

200

A Commentary

Joh. 1, 16. See Rom. 8, 29; Ephes. 4, 23; Col. 3, 10; Rom. 12, 2; Job. 17, 22; I Cor. 15, 49; Col. 3, 4; Phil. 3, 21; Or I Job. 3, 2.

From Glory to Glory.] That is: Either, by Communication of Glory to Us, from the Glory of Christ; according to that expression of our Saviour, fob. 17, 22, The Glory which Thou gaveest Me, I have given Them; and that of the Evangelist, of His Fulness have we all received: Or else, the Phrase may signify, from one Degree of Glory to another; In like manner as Ps. 84, 7, They shall go from strength to strength; And Rom. 1, 17, Therein [in the Gospel] the Righteousness [or Mercy] of God is revealed from Faith to Faith; from one degree of Clearness of Revelation, to another; from a less clear Dispensation under the Law, to a clearer one under the Gospel.

Even as by the Spirit of the Lord.] That is, (if the words be rightly thus rendred ;) All these things are accomplished in such a manner, in so wise, so effectual, so glorious a manner, as becomes the Dignity of the Great Agent, and are worthy the Operation of the Spirit of God. But the words [νυδιάτερ δήμωι πνέψε] may no less properly be rendred, as by the Lord who is the Spirit; namely by Christ, who in the fore-going verse is affirmed to be That Spirit the Apostle was speaking of, in the Sense which

on 2 Cor. 3; 17, 18.

which has been before explained. Which of the Two, is the Truer Interpretation of these last words; must be left to the Learned Reader to judge.

Your learned Friend, contends for quite another Interpretation of this whole Text; and thinks that the word, Lord, in the 17th verse, signifies not Christ, but the Holy Ghost: So that when the Apostle says, The Lord is That Spirit, his Meaning will be, The Holy Ghost is that Spirit. The reasons he gives for this Interpretation, are very dark and obscure; and make the whole Text very intricate, by a strange Confusion of Persons. That the word, Lord, in that place, cannot possibly signify the Holy Ghost, is (I think) very evident from the following considerations.

1. The whole Scope and Connexion of the Apostles Discourse from the beginning of the Chapter to the End, is very clear, natural and elegant, according to the Explication I have given of it above; taking the word, Lord, in the 17th verse, to signify Christ, according to the general style of the New Testament. Whereas, according to your Friends interpretation, the whole is made intricate and obscure, by a confused Transition from one person to another.

2. The Context immediately going before and following, necessarily determines the

201

202

## A Commentary

the word, Lord, in the 17th verse, to signify Christ. For in the 14th verse, the Apostle had said, that the Veil of ignorance, which is upon the Heart of the unbelieving Jews when the old Testament is read, is done away in Christ, that is, is removed when by believing the Gospel they are instructed rightly to understand the typical expressions in the old Testament. In the 16th verse he repeats the same Sense again in these words, When it [the Heart of the People of the Jews] shall turn to the Lord, the Veil shall be taken away: To turn to the Lord, that the Veil may be taken away; evidently means, being converted to Christ, [or embracing the Gospel,] in or by whom, he had said just before, the Veil is done away; And therefore when he goes on, ver. 17, Now the Lord —; it is most manifest that the word, Lord, must mean that very same Lord, who is spoken of in the 16th verse, and who in the 14th verse is expressly called Christ. Wherefore 'tis very wonderful your learned Friend should affirm, (pag. 134,) that Nothing occurs in the whole Context to determine the word to this Sense; when the very words immediately fore-going, do by most necessary and evident construction so determine it. And as to the words next following; far from confirming your Friends Interpretation, as he supposes; they also evidently show it to be

## on 2 Cor. 3; 17, 18.

be impossible to be true: For when the Apostle adds, Where the Spirit of the Lord is, there is Liberty; if the word, Lord, in the fore-going words, had signified the Holy Ghost, then the Spirit of the Lord would here have been as much as if he had said, The Spirit of the Holy Ghost, or the Spirit of the Spirit itself; which is manifestly impossible.

3. Lastly; Were there no other Argument, it would be sufficient to allege, that your Friends understanding the word, Lord, in this place, to mean the Holy Ghost; is entirely contrary to the whole Language of the inspired Writings, and tends to introduce the utmost confusion. For neither in the Old, nor in the New Testament, is there any one place, where the Holy Ghost is ever spoken of under the Title of Lord, but always distinctly under his own proper personal Denomination, the Spirit of the Lord, or the Spirit of God, or the Holy Spirit. There are only Two Places (and those your Friend seems not to have been aware of,) wherein Basil, contrary to all the Writers in the Ages before him, understands the word, Lord, to mean the Holy Ghost. The One is, 1 Thes. 3; 12, 13, The LORD make youto increase and abound in Love, — to the end he may stablish your Hearts unblameable in Holiness before God even our Father, at the coming of our Lord Jefus

203

## A Commentary

Jesus Christ. The Other Text is, 2 Thes. 3, 5, The LORD direct your hearts into the Love of God, and into the patient waiting for Christ. But how weak a thing it was in that Father, to think the Word, Lord, in these Texts, signifies the Holy Ghost; may appear by comparing these Texts with That other passage,1 Cor. 1; 7, 8, wherein the Name Jesus Christ is expressly added to the Appellation, Lord, in Both parts of the Sentence: — Our Lord Jesus Christ, who shall also confirm you unto the End, that ye may be blameless in the day of our Lord Jesus Christ.

# N° 41.

MATT. XXVIII, 19. Baptizing them in the Name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Ghost.

How This Text was universally understood in the Primitive Church, cannot be doubted; there being still extant a professed Paraphrase upon it, even the Apostles Creed; which from the earliest times of Christianity, was, with very little Variation in the several Churches, the Baptismal Creed, or Profession of Faith, which all Christians were taught on Purpose that they might

on Matt. 28, 19.

might understand what it was they were Baptised into.

To be baptized therefore in the Name [or, into the Name, eis 8 ovou,] of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Ghost; is, to be baptized into the Profession of our Belief,

In One God, the Almighty Father, [πα-

τεα πρυλοκεάτοεa, Supreme over All,] the

Maker of Heaven and Earth : And

In One Lord Jesus Christ, the only-begotten Son of God, who was incarnate, and died for our Sins, and rose again for our Justification, and now sitteth at the right Hand of God to intercede for us, and shall come again at the End of the World to judge all Men according to their Works : And

In the Holy Spirit of God, the Inspirer of the Apostles and Prophets, the Comforter of good Christians, and the Sanctifier of all Hearts.

Nothing can be more prapofterous, than, instead of thus comparing this Text with the antient Baptismal Creed, and with other Texts of Scripture; to reduce, on the contrary, the Words both of the Text and of the Creed, to the Standard of New Scholaftick Hypotheses.

Your Learned Friend says nothing at all upon this Head, but barely alleges Two Passages out of Antient Writers : Neither

205

of

206

## A Commentary

of which, have any the least relation to This Text; And One of them, is only a spurious Latin Sentence of Clemens Alexandrinus, who wrote in Greek. If it was genuine, there is nothing very remarkable in it: But what Regard is to be had to such Latin Scraps of Greek Authors, the Reader may judge from the very last Sentence of the Book, [Quis Dives etc.] to which This Fragment is annexed. For, the concluding Words, [ἀγαὶ τὴν πανδύς Ιποστὰ χεικὸν, ἡγαὶ αὐτὰ πνευμαίον, εἰπ δύξα,] To whom, THROUGH his Son Jesus Christ, and through the Holy Spirit, be Glory; are in the Latin Translation thus rendred, [Cui cum Filio Jesu Christo,] To whom, WITH his Son Jesus Christ, &c. And the like Translations are to be found in almost every Page of the Latin Versions of Greek Fathers.

## $\mathrm{N}^{\circ}$ 42.

1 JOH. V, 7. For there are Three that bear Record in Heaven, the Father, the Word, and the Holy Ghost; and these Three are One.

I observed that these words could not have any stress laid upon them in Any Controversy;

on 1 Joh. 5, 7.

troverly; First, because in the Original the natural signification of the words, these three are One, is, that they are One agreeing Testimony; (as very many Orthodox Divines among the Moderns, both Lutherans and Calvinists, freely acknowledge:) And Secondly, (which is much more material,) because the Whole Text itself does not appear with any Certainty to have ever been found in Any Manuscript Copy of the Original Greek at all.

The Learned Dr Mills alleges indeed, that the Text has been found in One Manuscript in England, in several of the most Antient Manuscripts in the Vatican Library, and in Seven of Robert Stephens's Manuscripts : But a Judicious Reader will easily perceive, that Every One of these are Mistakes.

How the Mistake arose concerning Stephens's Manuscripts, I have shown at large in my Letter to Dr Wells, pag. 43; and Dr Mills himself fully acknowledges it, in his Prolegomena, pag. 117.

As to the Manuscript in England, it is only a Book mentioned by a Foreigner, but which no Man in England ever heard of. And Erafmus himself, who is the only person that mentions it, declares at the same time, that he did not believe there was any such thing.

Concerning

207

208

## A Commentary

Vaticani antiquissimi & e mendatissimi aliquor, ad quos Hispani Theologi ediderunt Testamentum Complutenfe, in Quo habetur hæc pericope.

Concerning the Vatican Manuscripts,Dr Mills'swords are these. Several most antient and most correct Manuscripts in the Vatican Library, according to which the Spanish Divines published the Complutensian Testament, in which [viz. in which Printed Testament, not, in which Manuscripts] this verse is found. Now, besides that Dr Mills himself observes, that the Text is wanting in That most antient Vatican Copy, from which Sixtus Quintus's Septuagint was published; and that nobody else could ever find in the Vatican Library Any MS which had this Text, (as appears from Caryophilus's * various Lections collected by

*Totus septimus versus hujus Capitis desideratur in 8 MSS Codicibus Græcis. Sed quia citatur — a Cypriano, — & ab Athanassio in Disputatione cum Ario Nicæa habitâ, his verbis, Iωάννυς φάσκει, κὴ οἱ τεῖς τὸ ἐν εἰση, idcircò versus integer in Textu Græco relictus est, juxta VERITATEM LATIN/E VULGAT/E editionis, & IMPRESSOS etiam Codices Græcos. Caryophili varia Leß. ad finem Catene in Marc.

i. e. The whole seventh Verse of this Chapter (faith Caryophilus) is wanting in Eight Greek Manuscripts. But because it is cited by Cyprian, [which I have shown to be a Mistake,] and by Athanasius in his Disputation with Arius at Nice; [which is a spurious Book, and cites not this Verse neither,] in these Words, John faith, and these Three are One; therefore the whole verse is kept in the Greek Text [of Caryophilus's intended Edition,] according to the TRUTH of the Vulgar LATIN Edition, and also the PRINTED Greek Copies.

Note:

## on 1 John 5, 7.

by order of Pope Urban the Eighth; and as Erasmus has shown in his Note on the Text, and in his Controversy with Stunica; who, though himself concerned in the Complutensian Edition, yet alleges not against Erasmus Any Greek Manuscript, but appeals from the Greeks to the Latins and to Ferom:) an unprejudiced Reader would presently guess from the marginal Note in the very Complutensian Edition itself, that the Editors put in this Text upon the Authority of St Thomas Aquinas, who knew no Greek; and not from their Vatican Manuscripts.

Note: The Elight Greek Manuscripts, in which Caryophilus says This Text was wanting, were ALL the Copies which had This Epistle in them. For so he tells us in his Preface; [Conquititis jussi Sanctiffimi Domini nostri Urbani VIII, MSS codicibus venerandæ Antiquiratis, e Vaticanā potiflimum;—evangeliorum, decem; Actorum & Epistolarum omnium; OCTO; Apocalypseos, quatuor;] I collected, faith he, by the command of our most Holy Father, Pope Urban the Eighth, Manuscript Copies which were of the most venerable Antiquity; particularly out of the Vatican Library; of the Gospels, Ten Copies; of the Arts and all the Epistles, EIGHT Copies; of the Revelation, Four.

Tis also very observable, that in a Collection of various Readings from Sixteen Manuscripts, Eight of which were out of the King of Spain's Library, there is no mention made of This Text; though it was a Collection of such Readings as most favoured the Vulgar Latin, in order to correct the Greek Text by the Rule of the Latin. [Varia Lecciones Marchionis Veletii, Petri Faxardi, saftà collatione sedecim emplarium, in quibus erant Odo ex bibliotheca Regia beati Laurentii. — Plurima illarum savent impensè interpretati Vulgata nostra. — Magno labore comparata sunt a Viro Sapientissimo, or emendatus Græcus Textus ad Normam Vulgaei Interpretis.] De la Cerda Adversaria Sacra, cap. 91.

210

## A Commentary

Add to This, that the Text is wanting in all the Antient Versions; and moreover is never cited in the genuine Works of any Greek Father, either during the Time of the Arian Controverly, or before or after that time; though many of them cite the words both immediately fore-going and following this Text.

All This, your learned Friend (like a very fair and reasonable Adversary) readily allows. But then, as "it ought not (faith be) to be concealed, that This passage does not certainly appear to have been found in the Text of any Greek Manuscript; so it ought also to be told, that it appears to have been found in the old Latin Version that was used in the African Church: Else how could Cyprian and Tertullian have cited it?" I answer: Dr Mills shows, that the old Latin Version had it not: And therefore neither before Cyprian's time, nor for a long time after, was it cited by Latin Writers, any more than by Greek ones. Tertullian's words, [Qui tres Unum sunt,] are plainly the words of that Author himself, and not a Citation of This Text. The Author of the Book, de Baptismo Hareticorum, (allowed to be contemporary with Cyprian,) cites St John's words, agreeably to the Greek Manuscripts and the Anti-

ent

on I Joh. 5, 7.

211

ent Versions, thus: St John teaching us in his Epistle concerning our Lord, saith : " This is He who came by " Water and Blood, even Jesus Christ : Not by Water only, but by Water and Blood ; And 'tis the Spirit which beareth Witness,

Ait enim Johannes de Domino nostro in epistolà sua nos docens, Hic est qui venit per aquam & sanguinem, Jesus Christus; Non in aquâ tantum, sed in aquâ & sanguine; Spiritus est qui Testimonium perhibet, quia Spiritus est veritas. Quia tres testimonium perhibent, Spiritus & Aqua & Sanguis; isti tres in unum sunt.

because the Spirit is Truth: For there are Three that bear Record, the Spirit, and the Water, and the Blood; and these Three agree in One". In which Passage tis observable, that not only the whole 7th Verse, (as Dr Mills takes notice,) but the words (in Earth) in the 8th Verse, are omitted also: Which must be understood to be the Case, in most Books that want the 7th. From this manner of reading the Apostles words, both before, in, and after Cyprian's time, it is very improbable that Cyprian alone should have found the 7th Verse in His Copies. The Question then is; How comes Cyprian to say, It is written concerning the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit; these Three are [Unum] One? Of This, we have a clear Account given us by Facundus: John the

us by Facundus : John the Apostle (faith he) in his Epistle thus speaks concerning the Father and the Son and the Holy Ghost ; There are O 2

Joannes Apostolus in Epistola fuà, de Patre & Filio ac Spiritu sancto sic dicit; Tres sunt qui testimonium dant in terra, Spiritus, Aqua, Sanguis; hi tres unum sunt.

## A Commentary

postoli testimonium B. Cyprianus

Three that bear Record in Earth, the Spirit and the Water and the Blood; By the Spirit, meaning the Father; by the Water, the Holy Ghost; and by the Blood, the Son. Which Testimony of the Apostle St John, the Blessed Cyprian Bishop of Carthage, and Martyr, in his Epistle or Book which he wrote concerning the Trinity,

understands to be meant of the Father, the Son, and the Holy Ghost: For thus He writes; Our Lord faith, I and my Father are One; and again, Concerning the Father and the Son and the Holy Ghost it is written, And these Three are One: Thus Facundius. The Testimony of Fulgentius usually alleged on the other side, proves only that the Text of the 7th Verse began to come into the Latin Copies in His time, about the Beginning of the Sixth Century; which yet Facundus, who lived nearly at the same time, never heard of.

Thus have I gone through your Learned Friend's Book; not in the manner He went through mine, choosing out a Few of my Texts to discourse upon, and leaving the Rest untouched; but I have considered All the Arguments He alleged, and in the same Order

on I Joh. 5, 7.

Order that He alleged them ; without omitting any thing he offers, that Any Reader can think of importance.

I received at the same time a Second Reply from Dr Wells; wherein he so wholly refers himself to other Modern Authors, whose Arguments I had fully obviated in my First Book; and forbears entring Himself into the Merits of the Cause at all; and writes moreover in so particular a Style, and Manner; that I am perfwaded a Man of Your Candour and Fairness, and that understands True Reasoning so well as You do, will not think me under any Obligation to have any further Controverfy with Him.

I am,

the Trinity, by the Author of Some Considerations concerning the Trinity, and the Ways of managing that Controversy. In which, though, after the most careful Examination, I could find little more than a Repetition of the same Objections which I had already answer'd in the fore-going Papers; yet because those Objections were again set forth to particular Advantage, by the Skill of a very Able and Learned Writer, and by being proposed with a reasonable and good Spirit, I thought it proper to lay them before my Reader briefly, with the respective Answers to them.

An Answer to the Author

In the First place, I observe in general that This Learned Author alleges only some FEW Texts in Favour of his Hypothesis and takes no Notice at all of the greater part of those much more numerous Texts which are directly Against it; Not so much as attempting to show, how these latter Texts can be reconciled with his Hypothesis : And yet at the same time he is so candid as to declare concerning the Method I

took, that " the truest way of understand ing the meaning of Single Texts relating to the present Controversy, is to consider them ALL together in one view".

Secondly, I observe that All his Arguments through his whole Book, if they were conclusive, would prove directly, that the Son of God is God the Father himself; and consequently that Christ Our Mediatour is only a mere Man, in whom God the Father dwelt after an extraordinary manner: From whence it follows, that all the Worship paid in the New Testament to Christ our Mediatour and Intercessour, is either directed to God the Father dwelling in him, (which is supposing the Supreme Father to mediate and intercede with himself;) or else it is directed only to the Man Christ Jesus, (which is no less contrary to this learned Authors Hypothesis.) He complains that he did not fully comprehend My

## of Some Confiderations &c.

My whole Scheme : In what part of My Scheme, there is Any Difficulty of being understood, I am not yet aware; But This, I am sure, is an obvious, and, I think, an unswerable Difficulty in His.

But to proceed to Particulars.

OBJ. He objects (Pref. pag. 2; and Remarks, pag.92,) that Dr Clarke supposes 'the Divine Nature supplied the place of the

' Humane Soul in Christ.

ANSW. On which side soever That Question be determined, it makes no Alteration at all in My Scheme. And therefore, to avoid all needless Difficulties, I neither affirmed nor supposed any thing, which will not hold equally true upon Either Hypothesis. His inferring from these Words in my 38th Proposition, [the Divinity of the Son was personally and inseparably united to the Flesh,] that I denied Christ to have a Humane Soul; is a wrong Inference, unless the same can be inferred also from the like Words of St John, The Word was made FLESH; and of St Paul, was manifest in the FLESH; and of St Peter, Christ has suffered for us in the FLESH; and from his OWN Words, (pag. 98,) Christ's appearance in the FLESH,

## An Answer to the Author

Fig. 3.

OBJ. It is very consistent with Charity and Truth, to rank Dr Clarke in

- SOME Arian Clafs; because in five and

- fifty Propofitions there is but One Exprofes themselves Arians, would refuse to

- preflection, which any of those who now

- fubscribe to. *

ANSW. It is easy to demonstrate, that this Suggestion is by no means reasonable or well-grounded. The Body of Christians in the Primitive Ages, kept stedfast to that Form of Sound Words, the Baptistical Creed; which contained nothing in it, but what both Arians, and almost all other Seets whatsoever, were forced to acknowledge was the true and undoubted Doctrine of Christ: Is it from hence reasonable to suggest, that the Christian Church in the Primitive Ages was justly to be ranked under Some Arian Clafs, or under any Clafs of any other Seet whatsoever? This Learned Author will not suspect that it was. He therefore only is an Arian, who himself maintains, or imposes upon others, the particular Doctrines of Arius; Not he, who desires to adhere to those Antient and Scriptural Forms of Sound Words, which being from the Beginning unanimously received in the Catholick Church, had afterwards different Additions made to them by Men of different Seets and different Opinions, whereby endless Disputes were brought into the Church.

Difputes were brought into the Church.

But

## of Some Considerations &c.

But after all, What is being ranked in SOME Arian Class? Nothing but a Name of Dislike, without Any determinate Signification. Should I affirm, that This Learned Writer might with Truth and Charity be ranked in SOME Socinian Class, I think I should by no means act well in so doing: And yet His Scheme is less different from the Notions of Socinus, than Mine is from those of Arius.

OBf. ' Three Divine Beings——muft

' needs be conceived as Three Gods, notwithstanding any Subordination of the

' Second and Third Being to the First ; or

'elfe we muft free the Pagan World from

the Absurdity of Polytheism, and the

' Guilt of Idolatry ; these being generally,

if not always, founded upon a Subordina-

'tion of many Deities to the One Supreme.

ANSW. The Difference between Christianity and Paganism, is This. The Pagans acknowledged many FALSE (fictitious) Gods, and many FALSE (fictitious) Lords: On the contrary, Christians acknowledge only One TRUE God, and only One TRUE Lord or Mediatour. There are (faith St Paul) that are called, (that is, there were feigned by the Heathens,) Gods many, and Lords many; But to Us [Christians,] there is but One God, [viz.] the Father, Of whom

221

pag. 8;

1 Cor. 8,
5.

222

An Answer to the Author

whom are all things; and One Lord, [viz.]

Jesus Christ, By whom are all things. Now to say, that besides the One True God, there cannot be also One True Lord or Mediatour; is an Argument, not against my Scheme in particular; but 'tis the Argument which Deists use, (with what reason, I have elsewhere shown,) against Christianity in general. Or to say, that there is also indeed One True Lord or Mediatour, but that That One True Lord is the same Individual with the One True God; What is This, but to affirm in other words, that the One Lord, Jesus Christ, B T whom are all things, is the One God, the Father, O F whom are all things? Which is overturning the Apostles whole Argument, and introducing an absolute Confusion of Persons. Our One God, says the Apostle, is the Father: If then the One Lord, Jesus Christ, be That One God, whom the Apostle defines to be the Father, of whom are all things; is not this expressly affirming that the Son is the Father? Than which, nothing can be more hard to understand, or to reconcile with the whole Doctrine of Scripture.

But why must Three Divine Beings, of Necessity be conceived as Three Gods? One God, the Almighty Father; and One Lord, the Only-begotten Son of That Almighty Father; and One Holy Spirit of God, the Spirit of That Almighty Father; are in our Creed

## of Some Confiderations &c.

Creed represented to us as Three distinct Agents: And yet they are no more Three Gods, than they are Three Almighty Fathers, which is (according to the Creed) the Definition of God. One God, to whom Mediation is made; and One Mediatour, making Intercession for us to That One God, (which is St Paul's manner of speaking;) are no more Two Gods; than an Advocate with the Father, and the Father with whom that Advocate is, (which is St John's manner of expressing the same thing;) are Two Fathers. One Spirit, One Lord, One God and Father of all, who is above all; are by the Apostle represented to us, as Three distinct Agents: And yet they can no more truly be said to be Three Gods, than Each of them singly, (or than All of them together;) can be truly said to be The God and Father of All, who is Above All; Which is the Apostles Definition of the One Supreme God. Three perfectly co-ordinate, and equally Supreme Persons or Agents, (whatever Distinctions, or whatever Unity of Nature be supposed between them,) must of Necessity be conceived to be Three Gods, that is, Three Supreme Independent Governors of the Universe; because the proper Notion of God in Scripture, and in natural Reason also, as to all moral and religious Regards, is his being absolutely ὑπαντοκράτωρ, Supreme Ruler over All,

223

224

## An Answer to the Author

and ὀσκατὴρ πάντων, (Eph. 4, 6.) the Father or Author of all things: But, This Character being preserved entire, no other Power whatsoever ascribed or communicated to other Agents or Persons, can justly cause us to conceive more Gods than One. How and in what Sense the Son, though he be not That One God and Father of All, who is above All, may yet truly and properly be stiled God; has been largely explained in the fore-going Papers.

But now on the other side, if the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit, be conceived to be All but One Individual Being; it follows of Necessity, that the Son and Holy Spirit have no Being at all; Which is an insuperable Difficulty in This learned Authors Scheme. For if each of these Characters belong to One and the same Individual Being; and the Father Alone be (as is acknowledged) That Whole Being; it follows evidently that the Son and Holy Spirit, either are Themselves The Father, (which he is not willing to allow;) or else have no real Being, no Existence at all, but can only be Modes, Powers, Characters or different Denominations of That One Supreme, that One Simple and Uncompounded Being, which is the Father of All. The plain Consequence of which is, that our Mediatour and Redeemer is only a Mere Man, in whom God the Father manifested himself

## of Some Considerations &c.

himself after an extraordinary manner; and that the Holy Spirit is nothing but a mere Virtue or Operation of the Father. Which Notion, how much soever it may be defended, as an Hypothesis, by bare Reasen, (as may be seen in the Socinian Writers ;) yet I can by no means see how it is to be reconciled with what is taught in Scripture. Befides : Since this Learned Writer always supposes his own Scheme, to be the same with That which from the Time of the Fourth Century has been stiled Orthodox; it deserves to be remarked on the contrary, that by his plainly making the Son to be, not ὁμοθυσίς, but ταυπέσιος with the Father, that is, One and the same Individual Being; his Assertion in reality appears to be the same with That, which from before the days of Photinus to the Times of the Schoolmen, has by the Council of Nice, and all following Councils been condemned as Heterodox.

ANSW. That is to say : If the Authority by which the only Son of a Monarch administers his Fathers Kingdom, is itself the Authority of his Father, and not another Authority; then the Father and his Son are but One and the same Person under Two Denominations; there being no other Personal Distinction conceivable between them, but that of their being Two Men. The Argument is exaetly the same in every part.

OBf. ' Whatever is Eternal, must enter into our Notion of God: — Could Matter be Eternal, I should conclude it belonged to the Nature of God.

ANSW. Length of Duration, makes no Alteration in the Nature of any thing. God, is not only Eternal, but necessarily and independently so. Had Matter been produced by the eternal Operation of the Will and Power of God, it would not thereby have been a whit more Necessary or Independent, than it is when produced by the temporary Operation of the same Power and Will.

pag. 11. OBf. ' There may be Emanations Ne- ceffary, though not indeed Self-exifent,

from the One Self-existent Being.

ANSW. This, (besides that it is not well consistent with this Learned Author's own Hypothesis concerning Individual Being,)

can

## of Some Confiderations &c.

can very hardly be cleared from being a Contradiction. For whatever proceeds from any Being, otherwise than by the Will of that Being; doth not in Truth proceed from That Being, but from some other Cause or Necessity, extrinfick to and independent of That Being. Necessary Agents are no Causes, but always Instruments only, in the hand of some other Power. For which reason, the Antient Fathers both before and after the Council of Nice, do almost unanimously alert, that the Son was begotten of the Father, not by Necessity of Nature, but by the ineffable Power of his Will: As I have shown at large in my Scripture-Doctrine, Part II, §. 17. See also above, pag. 113.

pag. 11. OBf. ' There can be no middle Beings, between the Creator, and the Creature.

ANSW. This, if it be true, proves evidently the impossibility of this learned Writers own Scheme. For, the Creator signifying (in Scripture-phrase) the Father of all things, who created all things by his Son; if now there cannot possibly be any thing between the Supreme Father of All things, and That System of Beings which we call The Creature; it will of Necessity follow, that the Son of God must be either the Supreme Father himself, or else a mere Creature. To avoid this consequence, he is forced to suppose (pag. 129,) that the Son is something more than a mere Name, and yet not a real distinct Being; that is to say, that he is something between a Being and not a Being: Which is an undeniable Contradiction. I cannot but wonder, so learned a Writer should complain of My Scheme, for being what, after the most diligent Perusal, he cannot fully comprehend; and at the same time set up One in opposition to it, which of Necessity terminates either in a manifest Contradiction, or else in a Doctrine which of all others has always been esteemed the most Heterodox.

OBf. 'The Nature of That Being, who is Self-existent, may be communicated; though

of Some Confiderations &c.

though the Character of Self-existence cannot : Which is no more than to say, the Nature of the Father may be communicated, though the Paternal Character and Relation cannot.

ANSW. Self-existence is not an External and Relative Character, as Paternity is; but 'tis an intrinsic, absolute, and of all others the most essential Quality of the Substance or Being to which it belongs. If therefore the Character of Self-existence cannot be communicated, it follows necessarily, that neither can the Nature of the Self-existent Being, (properly and strictly speaking) be communicated; Self-existence being the principal Constituent of That Nature, or That which makes it to be That Nature which it is.

ANSW. Self-existence, and Existence it self, properly belong to Substances, not to P3 Powers

## An Answer to the Author

Powers or Attributes. Self-existence therefore is a primary, nay The primary and most essential Attribute of the Divine Substance. The Knowledge and Power of God, are not properly said to be Self-existent, but to be Attributes of Him who is himself Self-existent. The reason why the individual Knowledge or Power of God, cannot be communicated any more than his individual Existence, is because they are individual; and nothing that is individual, can ever be communicated from any one thing to another. Could the individual Knowledge of God, or the individual Knowledge of a Man, be communicated to another; there would be no Knowledge left in Him, from whom it was communicated. But the World well understands what is meant by Communication of Knowledge, Power, and the like: Could this learned Writer show as well how Self-existent Nature [or The Self-existent Nature] can be communicated, without an express Contradiction; this would be of great importance to him indeed.

An Answer to the Author lutely, and by way of Contradistinction, GOD.

pag. 15. OBf. 'Did the Ifraelites worship Mofes, because the Lord said unto him, I have made thee a God unto Pharaoh?

ANSW. Honour or Worship is always due in proportion to the Authority and Power, the Dominion and Dignity, of the Person to whom it is paid: And where there is no proportion between the Dominion or Dignity of Two Persons at any time compared together, there is also consequently no proportion between the Honours due to them. The Israelites honoured Moses, as being what he was, not as being what he was not: They honoured him, as a Servant, not as the Son, of God: as a Man authorized from God to lead the People, not as being himself a Divine Person. Christians likewise worship Christ, as being what he is, not as being what he is not: They worship him as being the Son of God, not as being God the Father himself: They worship him, according to his real Powers and Attributes, as being That Divine Person, who having been originally in the Form of God, voluntarily took upon himself the Form of a Servant, and was therefore highly exalted of God, and had a Name given him above every Name, that at the Name of Jesus every knee should bow, [every thing should be subject

## of Some Confiderations &c.

subject to His Dominion,] of things in Heaven, and things in Earth, and things under the Earth; and that [accordingly, in acknowledgment of That Dominion,] every tongue should confess that Jesus Christ is LORD, to the Glory of God the Father, Phil. 2,9.

ANSW. Not only not EVERY WHERE, but indeed not so much as in ANY ONE PLACE of Scripture, is Any One of these distinguishing Characters, viz. The One God, the Only God, the Lord God Almighty, o πανλοκεύτωρ, with others of the like import, ever given either to the Son or to the Spirit; but they are Always used as Synonymous to the God and Father of All, or the God and Father of our Lord Jesus Christ: As I have shown at large in the fore-going Papers, and in my Scripture-doctrine, by citing every one of the Texts in particular, wherein Any of these Terms are used.

are Titles expressive of Abstract Metaphyfical Attributes, which never occur in the

Sacred Writings, and have no Relation

ANSW. To be Supreme absolutely over all, is the English of the word παρὰκραπτρω; which does occur often in the Sacred Writings, and has relation to Us and to all Creatures. To be, absolutely speaking, the God of the Universe, is the English of ἐσως παρατήρ παραντων, οὔθιπαραντων, the God and Father of All, who is above All; which does also occur in the Sacred Writings, and has Relation to Us and to all Creatures. And they are not abstract metaphysical Attributes, but Attributes which express relative Dominion and Power, which are the Foundation of Morality and Religion towards God our Supreme Governour. Whence this learned Writer could be led into so strange a Mistake, I cannot imagine.

pag. 19. OBf. ' The Notion of God was fully

ANSW.

1

## of Some Confiderations &c.

ANSW. The Notion of God, so settled, was this, that He is the Father and Lord of All Things, the Author and Governour of the Universe. In which Notion, the revealed Doctrine of the Trinity has made no Alteration, nor made any other Addition to it but this, that the same God, the same Father, and Lord of the Universe, is now made known to us to be also in a particular and eminent manner, the Father [or the God and Father] of our Lord Jesus Christ. This Observation therefore proves the direct contrary to what the learned Author alleged it for.

OBJ. 'The God of the Jews, is likewise the God of the Christians; and

' where-ever the word, God, is ufed in the

New Testament, it is used in the same

' Senfe that it had in the Old.

ANSW. This Observation proves also directly the contrary, to what it is alleged for. For the God of the Jews, the God of our Fathers, (as St Peter stiles him, Acts 3, 13,) is the same who in the New Testament is stiled the Father of our Lord Jesus Christ. And therefore unless the Three Persons can be said to be the Father of our Lord Jesus Christ, neither can they be said to have been The God of the Jews, That God of our Fathers, who (St Peter tells us in the place now cited) glorified his Son Jesus.

The Texts of this fort, in the New Testament, are more than 300, as they stand collected in my Scripture-doctrine.

But

## of Some Confiderations &c.

But now on the other side, where the Word God is by the Construction necessarily determined to signify either two Persons or three, there is no one Text to be found. Which then of these Two, is begging the Question? To suppose that the Word God, when it occurs absolutely, is to be understood in the Same Sense, wherein it is acknowledged that of necessity it must by the Construction of the Text itself be understood in more than 300 other places of the same Book? Or, to understand it in a Sense, wherein there cannot be shown to be any Ground for understanding it in Any One Text? Besides: if the Word God, which always signifies an Intelligent and Powerful Agent, were ever made use of in Scripture to signifie what this Learned Author calls the Divine Nature, viz. the whole Three Persons, who are each of them also in Scripture always spoken of as Intelligent Agents; it would follow unavoidably, that the Divine Nature was a Fourth Intelligent Agent, distinct from, and constituted of, those Three Intelligent Agents. Which is the utmost Confusion imaginable.

All Power is GIVEN unto me in Heaven and in Earth. Matt. 28, 18.

The Father judgeth no Man, but hath COMMITTED all Judgment unto the Son. Joh. 5, 22.

These Texts expressly reveal to us, what Share Christ has under the Father, in the Government and Direction of our Affairs. The following Texts show as expressly, what Kind

him a Name which is above every Name; that at the Name of Jesus every Knee should bow, [every thing should be subject to his Dominion,] of things in Heaven, and things in Earth, and things under the Earth; And that [accordingly, in acknowledgment of That Dominion,] every Tongue should confess that Jesus Christ is Lord, TO THE GLORY OF GOD, THE FATHER.

In all these Texts here is a Worship paid to Christ, either as God, or as Man. If it is paid to him as God, yet it is not as Supreme; because it is expressly said to be TO the Glory of God the Father, and FOR his having redeemed us to His God and Father. But if it be paid to him as Man; then it is still more directly contrary to this learned Authors whole Hypothesis expressed in This Objection.

And to say that Christ is to be worshipped as the One Supreme God, whatever becomes of the personal Distinction of Supreme; is saying that Christ is to be worshipped as the One Supreme God, though the Father Alone be Supreme: Which is an express Contradiction in Terms.

But how it can be urged by such as believe there is also One Mediatour appointed by God, as well as that there is One God to whom Mediation is made; I understand not. ANSW. The Creation of a Human Soul, before the Creation of the World, before the Creation of Human Kind; is a mere Fiction without any Ground. A Human Soul formed more perfect than Any Angel, is a Human Soul created of a Higher Species than Human. The Notion itself, if it is at all intelligible, is perfectly the Arian Notion: The only difference is, that That which the Arians call a most excellent Created Spirit, is here stiled, much more improperly, a Human Soul. And to say we have no distinct Notion of any Perfections BETWEEN those which belong to GOD, and those which he hath imparted to MAN; is an Objection which (I humbly presume) needs no Answer.

21, Behold, I send an Angel before thee;—

ANSW. The words are spoken by God the Father: Not that the Invisible God and Father of all appeared, (whose shape no Man hath seen at any time, nor heard his Voice;) but the Father spake by his Son; that is, the Son spake in the Person (or as the Representative) of the Father: And the Angel there mentioned, is the Son himself. It is the

## of Some Confiderations &c.

the same manner of speaking, as occurs in All Writers, when Any One is introduced speaking as the Representative of Another, and mentioning Himself (as Grammarians speak) in the Third Person. There is no great Difficulty in the Expression; and yet Mr le Clerc (in his Notes upon the Place) urges it as an unanswerable Difficulty just in the same manner, though not with exactly the same view, as this Learned Author has here done.

OBJ. 'Is it possible for us to imagine, pag. 54

that all the Antient Patriarchs

should be mistaken in their Notions of

ANSW. The Jews and Patriarchs were under no Mistake in this matter. For they worshipped, not the Son, but the Father. The God whom the Jews and Patriarchs worshipped, St Peter stiles (Acts 3, 13,) the God of Abraham and of Isaac and of Jacob, the God of our Fathers: And This God, he says, glorified his Son Jesus. If then Jesus was the Son of the God of Abraham, Isaac and Jacob; it follows necessarily that the God of Abraham, Isaac and Jacob, was Q4 the

247

248

An Answer to the Author

the Father of our Lord Jesus Christ. And if so; then the God of Abraham, Isaac and Jacob, was not the Son; unless the Son was the Father of our Lord Jesus Christ: Neither was the God of Abraham, Isaac and Jacob, the whole Three Persons; unless the whole Three Persons were the Father of our Lord Jesus Christ. 'Tis True, the Person which appeared visibly to the Patriarchs, representing the invisible God and Father of All, was the Son: But then he is therefore called, both in the Old Testament and in the New, the ANGEL of the Lord: And in Him appeared the Glory of The Invisible God, whom the Patriarchs worshipped. If this was not the case; then it will follow of necessity, either that the Invisible Father himself appeared visibly, and was stiled an Angel of the Lord, Acts 7, 30, and elsewhere; Which I presume this Learned Author will not affirm: Or else it was an ordinary Angel which said to Moses, Acts 7, 32, I am the God of thy Fathers; And then his Objection returns with double force upon himself, Would God allow an Angel to take upon him the Style and Character, and to accept the Honour and Worship that belonged to the Father only?

OBf. If, when the Jews worshipped the Son who appeared, they had no No-

ANSW. Because the Christians have a Revelation concerning him, which the Jews had not. Christ in the Flesh, does not appear as representing the Person of the Father, but as a Person sent forth from the Father to be our Mediatour, Saviour and Redeemer; and who, distinct from the Father, has a Name given him which is above every Name; that at the Name of Jesus every knee should bow, [all things should be subject to His Dominion,]——and that every tongue should confess that Jesus Christ is Lord, to the Glory of God the Father.

God who is the Father of All, and as being Sent forth by him.

pag. 55. OBf. 'There is no true satisfactory

ANSW. That there is any thing in it—self not Rational in This part of this Learned Author's Scheme, (supposing the Father and Son to be nothing but Two Names of One and the same Individual, or that Christ was only a mere Man in whom the Divinity of the Father manifested itself;) I cannot indeed affirm. But there is This difficulty in it, that it is evidently contrary to the Doctrine of the New Testament. For there is expressly in the New Testament a Worship given to the Son, which cannot possibly belong to the Father; a Worship given to one Person, which cannot possibly be given to another, any more than one person can be another. For instance; When St John says, Unto him that loved us and washed us from our Sins in his own Blood, and bath made us Kings and Priests unto God and his

Rev. 1; fays, Unto him that loved us and washed us from our Sins in his own Blood, and bath made us Kings and Priests unto God and his Father,

Father, to him be Glory and Dominion for ever and ever; Is it to the Father in the Son, that Glory is here ascribed, for making us Kings and Priests to God and HIS Father? Again: When the four and twenty Elders fell down before the Lamb, saying,—Rev. 5 Thou art worthy;——for thou wast Slain, and hast redeemed us to God by thy Blood; Is it the Father in the Son, that is here glorified for being Slain, and redeeming us TO GOD by his Blood? And when the innumerable Multitude standing before the Throne, said, Salvation to our God which sitteth upon the Throne, AND to the Lamb; Is the Meaning of it, Glory be to The Father sitting upon the Throne, and to the Father in the Lamb? 'Tis very wonderful to me, that so Learned an Author should argue, just as if the New Testament had never been written at all.

## An Answer to the Author

ANSW. 'Tis somewhat hard to call That the Orthodox Doctrine, which (I believe) no one Christian Writer, for a Thousand Years after Christ, ever taught. But (which is more material)'tis also directly contrary to the New Testament. For Who is the Almighty Creator of the World, but He whom

St Paul calls The Father of All? And Who is the God of Abraham, Isaac and Jacob, the God of our Fathers; but He who (as St Peter affirms, Acts 3, 13,) hath glorified his Son Jesus; and whom our Saviour himself thus describes to the Jews, Job. 8, 54, My Father, of whom ye say that He is your God? If therefore the God of Israel was the Father of All, and particularly the Father of our Lord Jesus Christ, as both St Peter and our Lord himself expressly testify; how can this Learned Author affirm, that the God of Israel was (what he calls) the Divine Nature absolutely taken, without any regard to the personal Distinctions of Father, Son, and Holy Ghost; unless he will affirm that the whole Three Persons, Father, Son, and Holy Ghost, were the Father of all, and in particular the Father of our Lord Jesus Christ?

OBf. ' From what is Now discovered to us, we collect that it was the Son, and not the Father, who appeared as God in the Antient Times, and was worshipped as the

the One Supreme God, by the Jewish Nation.

ANSW. According to these words, the Jews did not worship God the Father at all but the Son only. Yet This seems, not to be this Learned Authors meaning: Only he introduces every where a Total Confusion of Persons. The Truth is; the Antient Jews did not worship the Son at all, but the Invisible Father only, appearing by his Son who is stiled the Angel of his presence.

OBf. In that passage, Joh. 10, 33,

Thou, being a Man, makest thyself God,

'tis plain the word God is taken absolutely,

and yet it cannot mean the Father, be-

cause the Jews knew very well that Christ

ANSW. As the Jews knew very well, that Christ did not pretend to be The Father; so neither did They nor his own Disciples then understand him to affirm himself to be the Son of God, in any such Sense as Christians now understand it: As appears evidently from his Disciples asking him even after his Resurrection, Acts 1, 6, Lord, wilt thou at This time restore again the [temporal] Kingdom to Israel? But, because he declared himself to be the Son of God, [the True Messiah,] in the Sense which he himself explains in the following Verses; therefore the Jews maliciously accused him of making himself God, just as Men say to an arrogant and presumptuous Person, you make yourself King. See this whole

## of Some Confiderations &c.

whole matter largely explained in the foregoing Papers, pag. 132, 144, etc. The Jews, whenever they mention God, always mean the Father: Joh. 8, 54, My Father, of whom ye say that he is your God. And if in the present passage the word had been used otherwise, (as it plainly is not;) yet it would not at all from hence have followed, that the word God in Other places signified any other than the Father; particularly in those Three Hundred Texts, wherein (as has been before observed) it is of necessity by the Construction itself confined to the Father only, as being expressly contradistinguished in those very Texts from the Son and Spirit.

in the fore-going Papers. But what LIKE-LIHOOD there was, the Jews should mistake Christ for the True God in this Learned Author's Senise; I cannot imagine. Our Saviour, far from doing nothing, did almost every thing in such a manner, as effectually to prevent his being confounded with God the Father. For he perpetually declares himself to be sent forth from the Father, to speak and act by his Commission and Authority; to do nothing of himself, but every thing by the Power of the Father; and nothing to his own Honour, but every thing to the Honour of his Father. And when he was accused of Blasphemy for pretending to be the Son of God, the True Messiah; the Answers this learned Author refers to as plainly allowing the charge, if the Son were not the Same with the Father; those very Answers (I say) which this Learned Author alleges, do most evidently disallow the Charge upon quite another foot. The Answers themselves are: Mar. 2, 10, The Son of Man bath Power, [Power from his Father; not, Power by being himself the Father,] on Earth to forgive Sins. Mar. 14, 62, I am the Christ, the Son of the Blessed: He does not say, he was himself That Person whom the High-Priest stiles The Blessed One; but the Son of the Blessed One. And Job. 10, 34, Our Lord, in answer to the same charge of Blasphemy for calling

## of Some Confiderations &c.'

calling himself the Son of God, thus replies : Is it not written in your Law, I said [concerning Magistrates] Ye are Gods; If be called Them Gods, unto whom the Word of God came, and the Scripture cannot be broken ; Say ye of Him whom the Father bath sanctified and sent into the World, Thou blasphemest, because I said, I am the Son of God ? See this whole Matter largely explained in the fore-going Papers, pag. 132, 144, 149 &c.

1 Cor. 10, 5, With many of them God was not well pleased, for they were overthrown in the Wilderness. "In all these places, (be says,) where the Word God is mention'd absolutely, Christ is meantly because whatever is here said of God, 'tis owned belongs to Christ in the Old Testament."

But indeed in None of these places does the Word God mean Christ, but the Father, according to the Analogy of the whole New Testament, as I have shown at large in my Scripture-doctrine. Neither do any of these things belong to Christ in the Old Testament, any otherwise than as he was the Angel of his Father's Presence, the Visible Person that there represented the Invisible God. And particularly in that Passage, Heb. ii, 3, The Worlds were framed by the Word of GOD, it is manifestly absurd to affirm (as this learned Writer does) that the Word [God] means Christ. For then the Apostles Assertion would be This, The Worlds were framed by the Word of the WORD.: Whereas indeed, in That place, not the Word [God], but the Phrase [The Word of God,] signifies Christ.

But that which sets this Matter past Dispute, is the plain Declaration of our Saviour, John 5, 23, that all Men should honour the Son, even as they honour the Fa- R 2 ther;

Fig. 61

260

## An Answer to the Author

- ther ; —— without fuggefting Any Distinction or Limitation thereupon.

ANSW. And does he indeed suggest no Distinction or Limitation thereupon? Tis very strange, a Learned and Judicious Writer should assert This; when both in the very Words immediately fore-going, and in those immediately following, there is an express Distinction or Limitation added, from the Reason and End of the Honour paid to the Son. The Reason or Ground of it, is, that the Father hath COMMITTED all Judgment to the Son: And the End of it, is, to the Honour of the FATHER which sent him. The Words of our Saviour are these: The Father judgeth no Man, but both COMMITTED all Judgment to the Son: That all Men should honour the Son, even as they honour the Father: He that honoureth not the Son, honoureth not the FATHER WHICH HAS SENT him. [The Expression is of the same Nature, as That in ob. 14, 1; Te believe in God, believe also in Me.] Thus likewise St Paul, Phil.2,10, That at the Name of Jesus every Knee should bow, [every thing should become subject to his Dominion,] and every Tongue should confess that Jesus Christ is Lord, to the GLORY of GOD THE FATHER. Besides: The Word [ἀνεύσαι, even us,] in St John's Stile, never signifies an exact Equality, but only a general Similitude. Thus Job. 17, 11, That they may

261

## of Some Confiderations &c.

be One, as [καθως] We are. Ver. 14, They are not of the World, even as [καθως] I am not of the World. Ver. 21, That They all may be One, as [καθως] Thou, Father, art in Me, and I in Thee. Ver. 23, Thou hast loved Them, as [καθως] Thou hast loved Me.

If this Text therefore sets this Matter (as this learned Author affirms) past dispute, I am sure it is past dispute on which Side it determines it.

ANSW. Worshipping Christ as being the One only True God, is not honouring the Son [also,] as [well as] we honour the Father; but tis honouring the Son, as being [himself] the Father. The Difference between the Worship paid to the Son in Scripture, and That paid to the Father; is not merely nominal or titular, such as arifies from the different imaginary Relations which One and the Same Being has to it self: But 'tis such a Difference of Worship, as arises from the real Difference of their R 4 Offices

## An Answer to the Author

Offices and Relation towards Us. Blessing and Honour is given unto Him that sitteth upon the Throne, [viz. to the Father,] upon account of his being θεός οὐσαντοκράτηρ, the Lord God Supreme Ruler over all: But unto the Lamb, standing in the midst of,[or before the midst of] the Throne, Glory is given upon account of his having loved us and washed us from our Sins in his own Blood, having redeemed us to God by his Blood, and made us unto our God Kings and Priests. The Glory given to the Son, is never founded upon such a Confusion of Persons, as this Learned Author every where supposes; but upon his being our Mediator and Advocate with the Father, and having a Name given him which is above every Name. And the reason our Saviour himself expressly gives, why he is to be honoured, as the Father is honoured; is, because the Father hath COMMITTED all judgment unto him, Joh. 5; 22, 23.

OB7. 'The most Primitive Writers—call

- Christ God, apply the Divine Attributes to

- him, and worship him as God, without

- Any Guard or Limitation.

ANSW. The Primitive Writers he here refers to, are Clemens, Polycarp, Ignatius, Barnabas and Hermas. Of which Five Writers, Four very seldom, if at all, stile Christ God. Ignatius only, does it frequently. But both He, and all the rest of them, far from speaking without Any Guard or Limitation, do constantly and with the utmost Care confine to the Person of the Father those Titles of Supremacy, τὸν δεσθώτην, τὸν δεσπίτην τῶν ολων, τὸν ἐπιταωσι γεον, τὸν κύειου γεον, πάντοκεαστῷ γεον, ιπὸν γεον τῶν ολων, [The Supreme Lord, the Supreme Lord of all, the God over all, the Lord God, God Almighty or Supreme Ruler over all, the God of the Universe,] and the like. And indeed, the plain Truth of this whole matter appears sufficiently from the Very passages This Learned Author has cited. For, of above Forty Passages which he has alleged out of these Writers, not One expresses the Notion which he cites them for; and several of them, express directly the contrary. As Clemens; when he stiles Christ, the HIGH PRIEST

265

PRIEST

pag. 67,
68.

266

## An Answer to the Author

PRIEST of all our Offerings. And Polycarp, when he thus prays; Now THE GOD and FATHER of our Lord Jesus Christ; and He himself who is our everlasting HIGH PRIEST, the Son of God, even Jesus Christ, build you up, &c. And Ignatius, when he says; There is but One GOD, who made Heaven and Earth and the Sea and all that are in Them; and One Jesus Christ, HIS only-begotten Son. And the Author of Polycarp's Martyrdom, when he faith,—glorifies GOD, even the FATHER; and blesses our LORD, the Governour both of our Souls and Bodies, and the Shepherd of the Catholic Church which is over all the Earth. And Hermas, when he says; The LORD has sworn by HIS Son, that whoever denieth his Son and Him, &c. It is not easy to express my Notion more clearly, than these most Primitive Writers have done it in these Very Passages, which This Learned Author has cited to prove the contrary. In what manner the Orthodox Writers in the following Age stiled Christ God, (besides the passages which I have cited from Justin Martyr in my Scripture-doctrine, Part II, § 36; and from Irenaeus, in the foregoing Papers, pag. 11;) appears from the following Passages of Terfollowing Paffages of Tertullian and Novatian. I will follow the Apostle, (fays Tertullian;) so that if the Father

ANSW. As to the Jews; this Learned Author answers himself in the very same page : The Jews (says he) in our Saviour's time had very strict Notions of the Unity of God; and therefore the Apostles, in their Discourses to Them, never mention any thing of this first Principle of Religion, but, taking

## of Some Confiderations &c.

king it for granted, insist wholly upon Christs being the Messiah or SON of GOD. What follows from hence, but that the Jews were taught to receive Christ as the Son of That One God, whom they already believed in? and consequently, that the God of the Jews, was no other than the Father of our Lord Jesus Christ? and that therefore there was no alteration made in their Notion of One God; but only they were further taught, that besides the One God, there was also One Mediatour, whom they were to honour and worship as such? As to the Gentiles; what They were taught to believe concerning Christ, St Paul tells us, 1 Cor. 8, 5, There are that are called Gods, whether in Heaven or in Earth, (as there be Gods many, and Lords many;) But to Us there is but One GOD, the Father, of whom are all things, and we in Him; and One LORD, Jesus Christ, by whom are all things, and we by Him.

Adv. Prax.

## of Some Confiderations &c.

Almighty Himself. Wherein this Opinion really differs from that of our Learned Author himself, is (as I now observed) very hard to show. But that it was not the Opinion of the greatest part of Christians, those plain and unlearned Christians in Tertullian's time, who perpetually cried out, [Monarchiam tenemus,] We contend for the Government of One God over the Universe; appears from hence, that Tertullian calls Praxeas's Opinion [novellitatem Praxeae hefterni] a Novelty of Yesterday; whereas the Opinion of the then greater part of Christians, was what he supposes to prevail always among the unlearned, from the Simplicity of the Creed itself which taught them to believe in One God; [Simplices quique, quæ major temper credentium pars est; quoniam & ipsa REGULA Fidei a pluribus Diis seculi, ad Unicum & verum Deum transfert.] What this Opinion was, appears clearly enough in the Writers before and about that Time; viz. Justin, Irenæus, Origen and Novatian.

OBf. ' It may be fairly replied, that pag. 94 x the word God, in all the paffages cited 95.

- in the Three first Sections of Dr Clarke's

- Scripture-Doctrine, ought not to be conbut is applicable to the Divine Nature

fined to the Perfon of the Father only;

considered absolutely, without any distinction S 2

275
